# TQM - New Chat Context

## Mission

TQM (THE Q-MODEL) investigates whether matter,
quantum behaviour and later gravitation emerge from
self-organizing oscillations of a temporal field.

## Current Core Hypothesis

Matter is not fundamental.

Matter consists of dynamically stabilized wave structures
inside a temporal field.

Synchronization and resonance are considered more fundamental
than particles.

## Current Research Path

Temporal Oscillators
    ↓
Synchronization
    ↓
Resonance Clusters
    ↓
Self-Stabilizing Wave Structures
    ↓
Proto Particles
    ↓
Quantum Behaviour
    ↓
Gravity
    ↓
Cosmology

## Important Decisions

- No cosmology first
- No gravity first
- No particle assumptions
- Emergence first
- Physics before interpretation

## Completed Experiments

### TQM-001

Synchronization emerges.

Result:
Order parameter R approaches 1.

### TQM-002

Random matrices do not produce convincing emergence.

Result:
Wigner-like spectrum.

### TQM-003

Structured topology alone is insufficient.

Result:
Dynamics likely more important than static structure.

### TQM-004

Field-mediated interaction alone does not produce synchronization
for two asymmetric oscillators.

Result:

The temporal field stores and transports energy but primarily
amplifies local frequency differences under the tested conditions.

New Insight:

The temporal field may behave more like a resonance medium than a
pure synchronization medium.

### TQM-005

Resonance cluster formation tested with 100 oscillators in a 1D field.

Result:

2 stable resonance clusters formed and persisted for ~19,000 iterations
(95% of the simulation). Each cluster contained 3 oscillators in a tight
4-5 cell region with stability score 1.0.

However, energy concentration was only 5.1% and oscillator participation 6.0%.
The field reached a steady-state density profile within 500 iterations and
remained static thereafter.

The hypothesis that matter = dynamically stabilized resonance structures
is SUPPORTED — stable clusters DO form. But they do not yet dominate the
field at current parameters.

### TQM-006

Critical resonance density in Kuramoto networks.

Result:

A SHARP phase transition was identified at ρc ≈ 0.09.
- Below ρc: transient clusters, R < 0.5
- Above ρc: persistent clusters, R → 1.0
- 20/20 (N,K) combinations showed the transition
- Higher K reduces ρc (K=5.0 → ρc=0.05)

The transition is a percolation-like phenomenon: once the random coupling
graph reaches sufficient connectivity, global coherent modes emerge.
This is the first TQM experiment to identify a well-defined critical threshold
for the emergence of stable ordered structures.

### TQM-007

Resonance family discovery above critical density.

Result:

5 resonance families were initially identified from 51 clusters across 60 parameter
combinations. However, TQM-008 later showed only F4 is truly reproducible.

### TQM-008

Reproducibility analysis of TQM-007 families (400 simulations, 100 seeds × 4 parameter sets).

Result:

Only F4 Stable Coherent is UNIVERSAL (74% occurrence, reproducibility score 0.721).
- F4 appears in 97-100% of runs at K ≥ 5
- F0 Transient: only at (ρ=0.30, K=3.0) — noise regime
- F1 Stable Large: NOT DETECTED — TQM-007 artifact
- F2 Ultra-Stable Compact: NOT DETECTED — TQM-007 artifact
- F3 Stable Medium: Seed Artifact (1% occurrence)

Best parameter set: (ρ=0.50, K=5.0) → F4 reproducibility 0.980.

Conclusion: TQM-007's apparent family diversity was largely driven by a single
seed. Only one robust, reproducible family exists at N=100 — the universal
coherent mode. Higher N or heterogeneous coupling may be needed for true diversity.

## Current Working Hypothesis

A ↔ Temporal Field ↔ B

instead of

A ↔ B

The temporal field is both a synchronization medium AND a resonance medium.

Confirmed: Stable resonance clusters form. A critical density ρc exists.
Only ONE universal resonance family (F4) is robustly reproducible at N=100.

Multiple families appeared in TQM-007 but failed reproducibility —
true family diversity may require larger systems or structured coupling.

Current emerging interpretation:

Resonance → formation of localized matter-like structures.
Synchronization → long-range collective behaviour.
Critical density → threshold for structure formation.
Universal family F4 → the single robust resonance mode at current scale.

### TQM-011

Perturbation stability analysis of TQM-010 condensates (25 runs).

Result:

CONDENSATES ARE STABLE DYNAMICAL ATTRACTORS — 96% survival rate.
- 24/25 runs survived perturbations up to 50% level
- Only failure: Density Reduction at 50% (catastrophic structural damage)
- Recovery time: exactly 249 iterations (first checkpoint after perturbation)
- Local R recovers to 1.0000 in ALL surviving cases — perfect self-repair
- Phase noise, frequency noise, oscillator removal, coupling reduction: 100% survival at all levels
- Fragmentation: 6 runs, Merger: 8 runs — perturbation can reshape but not destroy
- Mean lifetime reduction: only 30%

Proto-matter condensates are NOT fragile fluctuations — they are genuine
dynamical attractors that actively self-repair after severe disturbance.

### TQM-010

Local resonance condensation with 2D spatial embedding (64 parameter combos).

Result:

PROTO-MATTER STATES DETECTED — the first TQM experiment to demonstrate
localized coherent structures persisting in a globally incoherent background.

- 64/64 combos produced condensates (local R ≥ 0.80)
- 14 combos achieved proto-matter: global R < 0.5, local R > 0.80, τ ≥ 1000
- Multiple Clusters placement: best model — 5.12 condensates/run, global R=0.20
- Longest-lived: τ=4500 at N=500, λ=0.05, MultipleClusters, global R=0.28-0.44
- 29/64 combos have ≥2 coexisting condensates (multi-particle states)
- All placement models produce condensates but Uniform/Hierarchical tend toward global sync

Key finding: Local resonance condensation is the correct mechanism for proto-matter.
Structures emerge from local density enhancement, not global synchronization.

## Current Working Hypothesis

Matter is not fundamental. Matter consists of dynamically stabilized
wave structures inside a temporal field.

Confirmed: Local resonance condensates form stable, persistent structures
embedded in a globally incoherent background. These are proto-matter states.

Mechanism: Resonance → localized condensation → proto-matter structures.
Synchronization → long-range collective behavior between structures.

### TQM-012

Two-condensate interaction experiment (64 combos, 4 sep × 4 phase × 4 K).

Result:

Condensates overwhelmingly MERGE (80%, 51/64 cases) when within coupling range.
The remaining 13 cases (20%) are NEUTRAL coexistence at large separation (0.50)
with weak coupling (K=1,2).

- No attraction or repulsion observed — binary outcome: merge or coexist
- Merges occur at first checkpoint (250 iterations) — rapid phase-locking
- Critical separation: ≤0.25 (5λ) → always merges; 0.50 (10λ) → K-dependent
- Phase offset has minimal effect — merger dominates regardless
- Separation stays constant (no drift) — condensate positions are fixed by oscillators
- Final phase difference: ~0 for merged cases, ~2 rad for separated neutral cases

Key insight: Condensate interaction is a threshold phenomenon, not a gradual force.
Within coupling range → rapid merger. Beyond range → independent coexistence.

### TQM-044

Historical path dependence (8 sequences, 10 seeds).

Result:

AB ≠ BA — the ORDER of past experiences creates distinguishable identities.
4/4 paired comparisons showed significant differences.
Historical path dependence is a property of memory-bearing condensates.

### TQM-045

Historical identity lifetime (AB vs BA, 3 time scales, 8 seeds).

Result:

Path-dependent identity differences PERSIST — ΔR at 50K iterations
remains significant. Historical signatures do not decay to zero
on accessible time scales.

### TQM-046

Memory vs attractor competition (7 β × 5 times × 2 sequences × 5 seeds).

Result:

Memory (β) competes with attractor relaxation. Higher β preserves
identity differences longer, but attractor convergence eventually
dominates at long times for all β values.

### TQM-047

Identity–Energy Coupling Analysis (720 runs: 6 histories × 6 injections × 5 β × 4 seeds).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION D: Identity and Energy are INDEPENDENT properties.

- Pearson r = 0.0594 (negligible correlation)
- Mutual Information = 0.1275 bits
- Q1: Different identities CAN occupy the same energy (mean identity distance at fixed energy: 0.63)
- Q2: The same identity CAN occupy different energies (mean energy spread at fixed identity: 1.07)
- Q4: β weakly modifies energy levels (ΔE = 0.055 from β=0 to β=1)
- Q5: Energy alone does NOT determine identity — |r| = 0.06
- Q6: Identity and energy are INDEPENDENT state variables

Key insight: A condensate has at least TWO independent state dimensions:
identity (historical, structural) and energy (magnitude, intensity).
They are orthogonal degrees of freedom — not two names for the same thing.

### TQM-048

Identity Preservation Under Energy Transfer (1320 runs: 6 histories × 11 transfers × 5 β × 4 seeds).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION B: Identity partially follows energy within a single condensate.

- Critical threshold: ±25% energy transfer
- Within ±25% band: 98-100% identity preservation — identity is ROBUST
- Beyond ±25%: 0% preservation — identity is DESTROYED
- Overall preservation rate: 45.0%
- β (memory) provides negligible protection (44.7% → 45.5%)
- All histories equally robust (±1% variation)
- Baseline drift (0% transfer): 0.008 (near zero)

Key insight: Identity and energy are independent across different condensates
(TQM-047) but WITHIN a single condensate, identity survives only within a
finite stability band (±25% frequency change). Beyond this band, the attractor
landscape is reshaped and identity is lost. This reveals a STABILITY THRESHOLD
for identity — a condensate can absorb moderate energy changes while
maintaining its identity, but extreme changes destroy it.

### TQM-049

Identity Recovery After Energy Collapse (1296 runs: 6 histories × 6 transfers × 3 schedules × 4 β × 3 seeds).

Result:

IDENTITY IS FULLY RECOVERABLE — energy collapse is TEMPORARY SUPPRESSION,
not permanent destruction.

- Overall recovery score: 98.4% (D: Fully Recoverable)
- 97.8% of collapses achieve near-perfect recovery (>85% recovery)
- Mean post-recovery identity distance to baseline: 0.016 (near zero!)
- Collapse distances: 0.21 to 2.11 (fully destroyed identity)
- Recovery distances: 0.000 to 0.047 (nearly perfect restoration)
- ALL collapse magnitudes recover equally (97.5% at +500% to 98.9% at +50%)
- ALL restoration speeds work (Fast 98.6%, Medium 97.7%, Slow 98.8%)
- β provides minimal benefit (97.0% without memory → 98.9% with β=1)
- Memory survival during collapse: 79.6%
- All histories recover equally (97.6%-99.2%)

Key insight: THE ATTRACTOR LANDSCAPE IS ENERGY-DEPENDENT BUT IDENTITY-PRESERVING.
Large energy excursions move the condensate to a different attractor region
where identity appears destroyed, but returning energy to baseline RESTORES
the original attractor basin, and the condensate slides back into its
historical identity. Identity is ENCODED IN THE PHASE STRUCTURE and survives
energy excursions as a LATENT STATE. This is analogous to a memory that
becomes inaccessible but not erased — more like hiding than destroying.

### TQM-050

Identity Transfer Between Condensates (240 runs: 5 distances × 4 durations × 4 β × 3 seeds).

Result:

IDENTITY IS LOCALLY ISOLATED — identity does NOT transfer between condensates.
Close interaction produces identity DIVERGENCE, not convergence.

- 61.7% of interactions: Isolation (identities remain 100% distinct)
- 34.2%: Transfer with negative sign (identities DIVERGE, not merge)
- Only 4.1%: Hybridization or synchronization
- Transfer threshold: ≤1λ — beyond 2λ, complete isolation (0.0% shared)
- At 0.5λ: identities repel strongly (−67% A→B, −83% B→A)
- At ≥2λ: 100% survival, 0.0% shared identity — perfect isolation
- Transfer is symmetric (no dominance): −28% A→B ≈ −30% B→A
- β has minimal effect on transfer
- Duration does not amplify transfer (stable over time)

Key insight: IDENTITY IS REPULSIVE. When condensates with distinct
histories (AB vs BA) interact at close range, their identities DIVERGE
rather than converge. The coupling reinforces existing phase-structure
differences instead of averaging them. Identity is not contagious —
it is a LOCAL PROPERTY that resists propagation. This is a form of
IDENTITY EXCLUSION: distinct identities cannot coexist at close range
without diverging further apart in phase space.

Combined with TQM-012 (spatial merger at close range), this suggests
a nuanced picture: spatial positions merge (condensates physically combine)
but identities repel (phase structures diverge). The oscillator positions
may converge while the phase configurations become MORE distinct.

### TQM-051

Minimal Identity Formation (280 runs: 14 histories × 20 seeds).

Result:

NO IDENTITY QUANTUM FOUND — all tested perturbations are indistinguishable
from baseline. Identity is extremely robust against small historical changes.

- Noise floor: 0.202 (inter-seed variation within same history)
- Noise floor std: 0.337 (high stochastic variation)
- All 13 variants: identity distances 0.028–0.133 from baseline
- Minimum distinguishable threshold: 0.877 (noise + 2σ)
- 0/13 histories distinguishable — ALL fall within noise floor
- Amplitude variations (mean 0.061) produce larger shifts than
  timing variations (mean 0.040)
- Multi-pulse (mean 0.081) produces larger shifts than timing
- 3-pulse (most aggressive variant) only reaches 0.133 distance

Key insight: IDENTITY IS STOCHASTICALLY DOMINATED. The variation between
different random seeds for the SAME history (noise floor 0.202) is LARGER
than the systematic shift from perturbing the history (0.028–0.133).
This means: for small perturbations, you CANNOT reliably distinguish
a changed history from a different random seed. Identity formation is
dominated by initial conditions, not fine historical details.

Complementary to TQM-044 (AB ≠ BA for LARGE history differences),
TQM-051 shows that SMALL historical differences do NOT create
distinguishable identities. Identity has a "resolution limit" —
perturbations below this limit are indistinguishable from noise.

Identity has FIVE established properties:
- STATIC STABILITY (TQM-047): Identity ≠ energy across runs.
- BAND STABILITY (TQM-048): Identity survives ±25% energy changes.
- LATENT STABILITY (TQM-049): Identity recovers after energy collapse.
- IDENTITY EXCLUSION (TQM-050): Identities repel at close range.
- RESOLUTION LIMIT (TQM-051): Small perturbations indistinguishable from noise.
  Identity requires a minimum historical difference to be distinguishable.
  Below this resolution, identity is dominated by stochastic initial conditions.

### TQM-052

Conserved Quantity Discovery (144 measurements: 24 condensates × 6 transformations).

Result:

LOCAL COHERENCE IS AN EMERGENT CONSERVATION LAW — perfectly invariant
across all tested transformations (0.0% mean drift).

- Q7 Local Coherence: 0.0% drift — PERFECT conservation
- Q4 Global Coherence (R): 0.2% drift — conserved
- Q9 R × LocalCoh: 0.2% drift — conserved
- Evolution/phase noise/memory disruption: 0.0% drift on coherence
- Energy injection/removal: 0.5–0.6% drift on global R (minor)
- Collapse+recovery: 0.2% drift on global R (negligible)
- Classification: D — Emergent Conservation Law

Per-transformation breakdown for Local Coherence:
Evolve 0.0%, PhaseNoise 0.0%, EnergyInject 0.0%, EnergyRemove 0.0%,
CollapseRecover 0.0%, MemoryDisrupt 0.0% — ZERO drift under every
tested transformation.

Key insight: COHERENCE IS THE FUNDAMENTAL CONSERVED QUANTITY. Beneath
identity, energy, memory, and all other observables lies a single
invariant: the degree of phase alignment among oscillators. The
Kuramoto dynamics naturally drive toward and preserve coherence.
This is the "ground state" of the condensate — the quantity nature
conserves while identity and energy fluctuate around it.

Identity and energy are PROJECTIONS of coherence onto different
measurement axes.

### TQM-053

Causal Role of Coherence (168 runs: 6 histories × 7 coherence targets × 4 seeds).

Result:

IDENTITY AND MEMORY SURVIVE COHERENCE DESTRUCTION. Coherence is an
EMERGENT CONSEQUENCE, not the causal root — classification C.

- Identity preservation: 55-100% across all coherence levels
- Memory preservation: 81-100% — nearly perfect even at R_target=0.0
- Recovery score: 86-100% at all disrupted levels
- Identity collapse threshold: R ≈ 0.0 (no threshold found)
- Memory collapse threshold: R ≈ 0.0 (no threshold found)
- You CANNOT permanently destroy coherence — the system self-restores
- Achieved R never drops below 0.80 even for target 0.0

Key insight: THE CAUSAL TEST PRODUCES A PARADOX. Coherence IS conserved
(TQM-052) but you CANNOT meaningfully destroy it to test causality
because the Kuramoto dynamics are an attractor toward synchronization.
Phase disruption is temporary — the coupling instantly drives oscillators
back toward coherence. This means:

1. Coherence is not "caused" by identity — it's a DYNAMICAL ATTRACTOR.
2. Identity is built ON TOP of the coherence attractor.
3. The attractor cannot be broken by phase noise alone.
4. The causal question is ill-posed: coherence is not a variable
   you can independently manipulate — it's the system's natural state.

The relationship is HIERARCHICAL:
  Coherence (attractor) → Identity (projection) → Memory (encoding)
  
You cannot destroy the base without destroying the system itself.
The fact that identity survives "coherence disruption" is because
the disruption cannot truly destroy coherence — it only perturbs it.

## Next Open Question

What WOULD destroy coherence, and what would happen to identity then?
(If we could remove coupling entirely, or replace all oscillators,
would identity survive? Is there a transformation that bypasses
the coherence attractor?)

### TQM-054

Minimization Principle Discovery (1134 snapshots: 21 condensates × 3 phases × ~18 snapshots).

Result:

NO GLOBAL MINIMIZATION PRINCIPLE FOUND — classification A. TQM dynamics
are NOT gradient descent on any single scalar potential. However, the
RECOVERY phase IS a perfect minimization process (100% monotonic).

- Best candidate: P8 Coupling-Weighted Tension (52.8% monotonicity)
- All active candidates cluster at 52-53% — essentially random walk
- Frequency StdDev, LocalCoh Deficit: 0% monotonic (flat)
- Formation phase: 58% monotonic — weakly directional
- Energy injection phase: 0-2% monotonic — actively increases potentials
- Recovery phase: 97-100% monotonic — PERFECT gradient descent

Key insight: NO ACTION PRINCIPLE EXISTS for general TQM dynamics.
The phase space is not a simple potential well — multiple competing
forces shape the evolution simultaneously. But RECOVERY after
perturbation IS a minimization process: when the system returns
to baseline from a disrupted state, it follows the steepest descent
path toward lower potential values.

This resolves the TQM-052/053 paradox:
- Coherence IS conserved (052) — but not because it's minimized
- Coherence IS an attractor (053) — but not of a scalar potential
- The dynamics are RICHER than gradient descent — the phase space
  has curvature that simple potentials cannot capture
- Recovery is the SPECIAL CASE where minimization does occur

## Next Open Question

If there is no global minimization principle, what IS the geometric
structure of the TQM phase space?
(Is it a manifold with curvature? Are there saddle points? Can we
compute the Hessian of the dynamics to understand the local geometry?)

### TQM-055

Resonance Landscape Mapping (750 states: 6 histories × 5 β × 5 energies × 5 seeds).

Result:

MULTIPLE ATTRACTOR BASINS — classification C. The state space contains
5 major basins organized by ENERGY LEVEL, not by identity.

- 5 major basins: 143-149 points each, R > 0.98 stability
- Each basin corresponds to one energy scaling level (0.5-2.0×)
- Energy change ALWAYS crosses basin boundaries (0% same-basin)
- Local Coherence: ZERO variance — confirmed invariant

NOTE (TQM-056): These 5 basins were later found to be SAMPLING
ARTIFACTS — at 10× finer resolution, the landscape is continuous
with only ONE large basin.

### TQM-056

Emergent Basin Spectrum (1224 states: 51 energy levels × 2 β × 6 histories × 2 seeds).

Result:

CONTINUOUS LANDSCAPE — classification A. TQM-055's 5 basins were
SAMPLING ARTIFACTS created by coarse energy discretization.

- Large basin count: ALWAYS 1 across all clustering thresholds
- Optimal silhouette: 0.52 (single basin), optimal threshold: 1.0
- Total basin count varies 6-152 depending on threshold (fragmentation)
- Large basin count STABLE at exactly 1 — robust to method
- Energy vs R: nearly flat, all levels >0.92 (no forbidden zones)
- 47/51 energy levels have R > 0.90 (preferred zones everywhere)
- No discrete basin boundaries emerge from continuous sampling

Key insight: THE RESONANCE LANDSCAPE IS A SINGLE CONNECTED ATTRACTOR.
Energy is a CONTINUOUS GRADIENT, not a source of discrete basins.
The 5 basins in TQM-055 were artifacts of using only 5 discrete
energy levels — exactly H0 was correct. At fine resolution, all
energy levels form coherent states (R > 0.92) in one continuous basin.

This is an important methodological result: discrete sampling grids
can CREATE artificial basin structures. The true topology is smooth
and connected. Energy acts as a continuous organizing dimension
within a single global attractor.

### TQM-057

Resonance State-Space Geodesics (36 trajectories: 4 histories × 3 seeds × 3 perturbations).

Result:

DETERMINISTIC GEODESIC PATHS — classification D. Recovery follows
near-geodesic trajectories with 89.4% repeatability.

- Repeatability: 89.4% — paths are highly deterministic
- Shortest path ratio: 1.33× — near-geodesic (direct path ≈ 75% of actual)
- Path lengths: EnergyCollapse 0.10, PhaseNoise 0.03, MemoryDisrupt 0.41
- Convergence: 238-488 iterations to reach baseline neighborhood
- Curvature ~120 rad accumulated over 2000 iters (small oscillations)
- R recovers from perturbed to ~0.99 within 50-200 iterations
- All perturbation types follow similar recovery geometry

Key insight: THE RESONANCE LANDSCAPE HAS AN INTRINSIC METRIC. State
transitions follow preferred geodesic paths that are deterministic,
repeatable, and near-minimal in length. This means the state space
is not just a connected attractor — it has a well-defined GEOMETRY
where recovery is a gradient flow along the steepest descent.

This explains TQM-054's finding (recovery is a minimization process):
the geometry of the landscape guides trajectories along the shortest
path to the attractor. The 120 rad curvature with short path length
suggests the system takes a nearly-straight path with small-scale
spiraling — like a gyre converging to a drain.

### TQM-058

Resonance Curvature and Effective Gravity (12 geodesic deviation pairs: 4 magnitudes → 6 pairs).

Result:

GEOMETRICALLY DOMINATED — classification D. The state space has strong
intrinsic curvature that creates effective "gravitational" attraction.

- 100% of trajectory pairs CONVERGE (final separation always 0.0)
- Mean curvature magnitude: 0.079 (geometrically significant)
- Mean convergence rate: 0.266 per iteration
- Mean separation change: −0.017 (from 0.01−0.03 to 0.0)
- Curvature sign: sphere-like (trajectories focus)
- Convergence/curvature ratio: 3.37× (convergence tracks curvature)

Key insight: THE STATE SPACE IS A CURVED MANIFOLD. Nearby trajectories
converge like geodesics on a sphere, creating effective attraction
without any explicit "force." This is a geometric analog of gravity:
CURVATURE, not external forces, drives state transitions toward the
attractor. The resonance landscape is not just connected and geodesic —
it is intrinsically curved, and that curvature creates the effective
dynamics that TQM-054 described as "minimization."

This provides the geometric FOUNDATION for all prior TQM findings:
- Recovery (TQM-049) = geodesic flow on a curved manifold
- Minimization (TQM-054) = gradient along curvature
- Geodesics (TQM-057) = shortest paths in curved geometry
- Effective gravity (TQM-058) = focusing of geodesics by curvature

### TQM-059

Sources of Resonance Curvature (240 pairs: 3 scans × 40 points × 6 pairs).

Result:

MEMORY DOMINATED — classification E. Memory strength (β) is the primary
curvature generator with r = 0.932 correlation. Energy and coherence
contribute negligibly.

- Memory-curvature correlation: r = **0.932** (near-perfect)
- Energy-curvature correlation: r = −0.029 (zero)
- Identity curvature variance: σ = 0.045 (moderate)
- 100% convergence across ALL scan points
- Curvature range: 0.045 (β=0.05) → 0.202 (β=2.0)
- Higher β = stronger curvature = faster convergence
- Energy has NO effect on curvature (flat across 0.0–5.0)

Key insight: MEMORY IS THE GEOMETRIC ARCHITECT. The β parameter —
which controls how strongly past phase relationships influence
current dynamics — is what CURVES the state space. Without memory
(β→0), the space is flatter; with strong memory (β=2), curvature
is maximal and trajectories converge fastest.

This provides the CAUSAL CHAIN:
  Memory (β) → Curvature → Effective gravity → Recovery dynamics

TQM-044 showed memory creates identity (AB ≠ BA).
TQM-059 shows memory creates CURVATURE — it is the geometric
source of all effective dynamics in the resonance state space.

### TQM-060

Memory–Curvature Feedback Loop (14 profiles: 7 β × 2 seeds, 50 cycles each).

Result:

NO FEEDBACK — classification A. Memory (β) creates curvature, but
curvature does NOT feed back to create more memory. The causal arrow
is ONE-WAY.

- Mean feedback coefficient: 0.058 (negligible)
- Memory growth over 50 cycles: ~0.001–0.005 (stable)
- Curvature FLUCTUATES (±0.05) but does NOT grow systematically
- β-feedback correlation: r = −0.12 (no trend)
- 7/14 profiles show saturation (curvature reaches steady state)
- No critical β for self-reinforcement
- H0 confirmed: one-way causation only

Key insight: THE GEOMETRY IS FIXED. Memory (β) determines curvature
as a STATIC property of the system — it is not plastic or self-modifying
under repeated experience. The condensate's memory parameter creates
a fixed geometric structure; curvature does not feed back to grow
more memory. The system is geometrically STABLE.

The complete causal picture is now:
  Memory (β) → Curvature → Effective gravity → Recovery dynamics
  (fixed)      (static)     (deterministic)    (geodesic)

This is a ONE-WAY causal chain with no feedback amplification.
The geometry is innate, not learned through experience.

### TQM-061

Spontaneous Memory Emergence (5 profiles: β=0 with 10/50/100 cycles + β=0.5 controls).

Result:

MEMORY IS PURELY EXTERNAL — classification A. At β=0, absolutely zero
path dependence emerges regardless of experience cycle count.

- Path dependence at β=0: 0.000000 for ALL cycle counts
- Path dependence at β=0.5: 0.000000 (even controls show zero)
- Identity persistence: 100% at all β (identity always recovers)
- No curvature emerges without explicit β
- 100 cycles of AB/BA training at β=0 produce identical final states
- H0 confirmed: memory is purely external, not emergent

Key insight: MEMORY CANNOT EMERGE FROM REPEATED EXPERIENCE ALONE.
The β parameter is ESSENTIAL — without it, AB and BA training
sequences produce identical final states regardless of how many
cycles are applied. The ordering effect (AB ≠ BA) discovered in
TQM-044 requires explicit memory encoding via β > 0.

This is the definitive answer to the emergence question: memory
is a FUNDAMENTAL PARAMETER of the Kuramoto dynamics, not an
emergent property. It must be explicitly provided; it cannot
arise naturally from phase perturbations alone.

### TQM-062

State-Space Curvature to Spatial Motion (20 sims: 10 β pairs × 2 seeds).

Result:

EFFECTIVE ATTRACTION FROM GEOMETRY — classification D, but from
COUPLING dynamics, not curvature. Phase-aligned oscillators attract
spatially via the Kuramoto coupling gradient.

- 19/20 simulations converge (95%)
- Separation: 0.40 → 0.06 over 3000 iterations
- Groups synchronize internally (R→1.0) in ~100 iterations
- Post-sync: mutual spatial attraction pulls groups together
- β-drift correlation: r = −0.03 — ZERO (curvature irrelevant)
- All β pairs converge equally regardless of memory strength

Key insight: MOTION FROM COUPLING, NOT CURVATURE. Once oscillators
synchronize, the phase-alignment gradient (cos(Δθ)) creates
attractive spatial forces via the coupling potential. This is
geometric but from the coupling energy landscape, not from
β-mediated state-space curvature. The mechanism is:
sync → phase alignment → coupling gradient → spatial convergence.

### TQM-063

Resonance Error Reduction Dynamics (32 profiles: fixed/moving × 4 separations × 2 identities × 2 seeds).

Result:

FORCE DRIVEN — classification A. Resonance error does NOT drive motion;
it INCREASES as condensates attract. Motion comes from coupling forces.

- Error INCREASES in both fixed (−0.0003/step) and moving (−0.0003/step)
- Error-motion correlation: r = −0.08 (zero)
- Identity mismatch does NOT affect error reduction
- Motion does NOT minimize error — it increases it
- Fixed positions: error grows as groups internally synchronize
- Moving positions: error grows faster as groups converge
- ΔSep: −0.03 to −0.25 (significant attraction in moving mode)

Key insight: MOTION IS NOT ERROR MINIMIZATION. Attraction pulls
condensates together, but this motion INCREASES resonance error —
oscillators are pulled out of locally-optimal phase configurations.
Error is a CONSEQUENCE of motion, not its CAUSE. The system does
not naturally minimize resonance error (consistent with TQM-054:
no global minimization principle).

The causal chain is: coupling forces → spatial motion → error increase.
Not: error gradient → error minimization → spatial motion.

### TQM-064

Universality of Effective Attraction (32 runs: 8 coupling laws × 2 separations × 2 seeds).

Result:

UNIVERSAL CONSEQUENCE OF SYNCHRONIZATION — classification D. Spatial
attraction persists across 7/8 coupling laws tested. Only anti-symmetric
sin(Δθ) produces divergence at large separations.

- 91% convergence across all laws (29/32 runs)
- 100% synchronization across ALL laws
- Always-positive laws (cos², exp, 1/(1+|x|), 1-|x|/π): 100% convergence
- sin(Δθ): diverges at 2λ (+0.15), converges at 0.5λ (−0.02)
- Attraction-sync correlation: r = 0.11 (weak — not proportional)
- 3/32 synchronized pairs do NOT converge
- K8 (1-|Δθ|/π): weakest attraction (51%), marginal at small sep

Key insight: ATTRACTION IS A ROBUST CONSEQUENCE OF SYNCHRONIZATION.
Any coupling law that is predominantly positive (promoting alignment)
produces spatial convergence. Only anti-symmetric laws that can
produce repulsive forces break the pattern. Attraction is NOT
specific to cos(Δθ) — it is a universal geometric property of
synchronization-promoting coupling dynamics.

### TQM-065

Coupling Symmetry Principle (70 profiles: 11 named + 24 sweep combinations × 2 seeds).

Result:

FUNCTION SPECIFIC — classification B. Even symmetry guarantees
attraction (100% convergence), but odd symmetry does NOT guarantee
repulsion. Odd laws attract at close range through sync dynamics.

- Even laws: 100% convergence across all laws
- Odd laws: 75% convergence at 1λ (sin, sin³, tanh all attract!)
- Even-attraction r = 0.48, Odd-attraction r = −0.49 (moderate)
- Automated sweep: evenW≥0.25 → 100% convergence
- EvenW=0 (pure sin): 75% still converge (75% attraction score)
- Distance dependence: sin attracts at 1λ, repels at 2λ (TQM-064)
- Classification: B — not a universal symmetry principle

Key insight: SYMMETRY IS NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT. Even symmetry
GUARANTEES attraction, but odd symmetry does not guarantee repulsion.
At close range, synchronization dynamics overpower anti-symmetric
force components, producing net attraction even for odd laws.
The simple even→attract, odd→repel rule holds at LARGE separations
but breaks at CLOSE range where coupling is strong.

The universal pattern is:
  Close range (≤1λ): ALL laws attract (sync dominates)
  Far range (≥2λ): Only even laws attract; odd laws may repel

### TQM-066

Critical Coupling Universality (320 runs: 8 laws × 10 K × 2 sep × 2 seeds).

Result:

NO UNIVERSALITY — classification A. No critical Kc exists. Coupling
law matters at ALL strength levels. Variance across laws: 25-63%.

- Law variance: 25-63% across all K (never drops below significance)
- Classification: A — No Universality
- No phase transition or sharp crossover detected
- Synchronization COLLAPSES at K=10 (3%) — paradoxically
- sin produces REPULSION at low K (−37% attraction)
- cos², exp, 1/(1+|x|): 94-100% attraction even at low K
- Peak law variance at K=2.0 (63%) — maximum law dependence
- All laws remain DISTINCT at every K level

Key insight: THE COUPLING LAW ALWAYS MATTERS. There is no regime
where "strong enough coupling" makes the detailed force law
irrelevant. Even at extreme K=10, different force laws produce
25% variance in attraction — fundamentally different behaviors.
The system is INHERENTLY LAW-DEPENDENT.

Surprisingly, very high coupling (K=10) DESTROYS synchronization
(sync drops to 3%) — the position dynamics with strong coupling
create chaotic motion that prevents global coherence. Universal
behavior does not emerge; instead, the system transitions from
coherent to disordered at extreme K.

### TQM-067

Overcoupling Instability (45 runs: 15 K × 3 seeds, N=200, fixed positions).

Result:

NO SYNCHRONIZATION COLLAPSE in pure Kuramoto. TQM-066's collapse at
K=10 was caused by POSITION DYNAMICS, not by high coupling. Pure
Kuramoto sync remains stable (R>0.98) from K=1 to K=50.

- Sharp phase transition at K≈0.5–1.0 (classic Kuramoto threshold)
- K<0.5: R≈0.07 (incoherent), K≥1: R≥0.89 (synchronized)
- Peak R=0.9996 at K=20 (optimal coupling)
- K=100: R=0.78 (mild degradation, possible numerical effects)
- No overcoupling collapse in pure Kuramoto
- Fixed-position vs moving-position: dramatic difference

Key insight: TQM-066's sync collapse was a POSITION-DYNAMICS EFFECT,
not a coupling-strength effect. When oscillators have FIXED positions
(pure Kuramoto), synchronization is robust from K=1 to K=50+.
The collapse at K=10 in TQM-066 was caused by coupled phase-position
dynamics creating spatial chaos at high coupling.

### TQM-068

Curvature Coupling to Spatial Dynamics (14 runs: 7 β × 2 seeds,
N=100, β = 0..2, full geodesic deviation curvature measurement).

Result:

NO COUPLING between curvature and spatial motion.

- β → curvature: r = 0.7084 (strong — confirms TQM-059)
- curvature → velocity: r = 0.0546 (negligible)
- curvature → acceleration: r = -0.0668 (negligible)
- curvature → convergence: r = 0.0371 (negligible)
- β → drift: r = -0.2304 (weak)
- Classification: A: No Coupling

Key insight: Memory (β) strongly generates curvature in the state
space, but this curvature has ZERO measurable influence on spatial
dynamics. Trajectories are essentially identical regardless of β —
condensates converge to the midpoint at the same rate whether
curvature is high or low. Curvature is a geometric epiphenomenon:
it is generated by memory but does not feed back into spatial
motion. Spatial dynamics are governed by coupling gradients
(phase alignment), not by the intrinsic curvature of the state
space.

This is a scientifically important negative result: it establishes
that curvature and motion are independent phenomena in the
Kuramoto framework.

### TQM-069

Coupling Information Principle (216 runs: 8 named + 100 random
functions × 2 seeds, N=100, 2000 iters each).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION B: WEAK PREDICTOR — no universal coupling principle.

Top 3 predictors of attraction:
1. PositiveArea: r = +0.3762 (14% variance explained)
2. AreaRatio: r = +0.3677
3. MeanValue: r = +0.3634

- Symmetry alone: r = +0.0648 (NEGLIGIBLE — confirms TQM-065)
- Entropy: r = -0.0842 (negligible — information theory does not help)
- No single static property strongly predicts attraction

Key insight: The POSITIVE AREA of the coupling function is the
dominant predictor — functions with more net positive coupling
produce stronger attraction. cos² (100% positive area) → 84%
attraction; sin (50% balanced area) → 33% attraction. However,
the correlation is modest (r≈0.38), meaning attraction is
primarily a DYNAMICAL phenomenon that emerges from the
interaction between coupling and phase evolution, not from
any static mathematical property of the coupling function alone.

This is consistent with TQM-066 (no universal coupling regime),
TQM-065 (symmetry alone insufficient), and TQM-068 (curvature
does not drive motion). Attraction appears to be an emergent
property of the coupled phase-position system.

### TQM-070

Onset of Attraction (9 runs: 3 laws × 3 seeds, N=100,
3000 iters, per-timestep tracking).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION B: ATTRACTION FOLLOWS SYNC.

- Mean R at onset: 0.8366 (high synchronization required)
- Mean coherence at onset: 0.9430
- Mean phase variance at onset: 0.1634
- 2/9 runs show attraction before full sync (22%)
- Sharp phase transition at R≈0.875–0.925

Key insight: Attraction is a POST-SYNCHRONIZATION phenomenon.
The temporal sequence is:
1. Oscillators synchronize phases (R→1, coherence→1)
2. THEN spatial attraction begins (separation decreases)
3. Transition is SHARP (sensitivity ~983 per ΔR=0.01)

R-bin analysis:
- R<0.875: attraction < 1% (negligible)
- R≈0.925: attraction ~32%
- R≈0.975: attraction ~65%

Phase synchronization is the prerequisite for spatial attraction.
Without phase coherence, coupling forces cancel and produce no
net spatial motion. Only after oscillators synchronize can they
exert coherent forces on each other's positions.

This confirms that attraction is a DYNAMICAL phenomenon that
emerges from the interplay of phase and position evolution,
not from static coupling function properties.

### TQM-071

Critical Coherence Threshold (279 controlled-R measurements:
31 points × 3 laws × 3 seeds, N=100, von Mises state preparation,
30-step instantaneous attraction probe).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION A: NO THRESHOLD.

- Attraction probability: 100% at ALL R from 0.70 to 1.00
- Mean attraction force: ~0.015, nearly constant across R
- No R_crit detected — attraction exists at every coherence level
- Transition type: Continuous (no phase transition)
- Independent of coupling law (cos, cos², exp all identical)
- Independent of β

Key insight (CRITICAL): This APPARENTLY contradicts TQM-070.
Resolution:
- TQM-070: NATURAL dynamics → sync first, then attraction
- TQM-071: ARTIFICIALLY imposed coherence → attraction at all R

The coupling force law ALWAYS produces attractive forces when
there is ANY phase coherence. The "threshold" observed in
TQM-070 is a DYNAMICAL threshold — when sync naturally becomes
strong enough (R≈0.875) for attraction to dominate over noise.

But the force law itself has NO intrinsic threshold. If you
externally prepare oscillators with R=0.70, they attract
immediately.

This is a profound finding: the absence of attraction at low R
in natural dynamics is NOT because the force law requires high
R, but because the natural dynamics haven't yet produced enough
coherence. Attraction is always latent; synchronization merely
unlocks it.

### TQM-072

Coherent Force Summation (88 force profiles: 11 R-points ×
4 laws × 2 seeds, 220,000 pair forces analyzed).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION D: COHERENT SUMMATION DOMINATED.

- r(alignment, R):       0.9342  (coherence → alignment)
- r(cancellation, R):    0.9637  (coherence → less cancellation)
- r(net force, alignment): 0.9861 (alignment → net force)
- R² = 0.9723 (97% variance explained)

Direction histograms reveal the mechanism:
- R=0: bimodal — 50% forces toward B, 50% away → cancellation
- R=1: unimodal — 100% forces toward B → perfect alignment

Per-law cancellation ratios:
- cos:   0.014 (R=0) → 0.995 (R=1)  [70x improvement]
- sin:   stays near 0 at all R        [never aligns]
- cos²:  ~0.994 at all R             [always positive, no cancellation]
- exp:   ~0.994 at all R             [always positive, no cancellation]

Key insight: THIS IS THE MECHANISM. Attraction is entirely a
vector summation effect:
1. Local forces exist at all R (cos is positive near Δθ=0)
2. At low R: Δθ varies randomly → forces point in all directions
   → vector sum cancels → small net force
3. At high R: Δθ ≈ 0 for all pairs → all forces point toward B
   → vector sum adds coherently → large net force

Coherence is a FORCE-ALIGNMENT mechanism. It does not create
attraction — it prevents cancellation. The sin law confirms:
sin(0)=0 so even R=1 produces no force. The always-positive
laws (cos², exp) confirm: they never cancel, so attraction is
constant across R.

This resolves TQM-070/071: attraction requires sync because
sync eliminates the cancellation that random phases create.

### TQM-073

Analytic Force Emergence Law (408 data points: 51 R-levels ×
4 laws × 2 seeds, 9 candidate models tested).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION B: APPROXIMATE EMPIRICAL LAW.

Per-law R² (Poly5, best model):
- cos:         0.9652  (excellent)
- exp(-|x|):   0.9089  (excellent)
- cos²:        0.8139  (good)
- 1/(1+|x|):   0.7521  (good)

Combined R²: 0.6140 (laws mixed — different F_max per law).

Model ranking (combined):
1. Poly5:  R²=0.614  (best — universal analytic form)
2. Poly4:  R²=0.609
3. Poly3:  R²=0.609
4. Logistic: R²=0.466
5. Power:  R²=0.413
6. Exponential: R²=0.387
7. Linear: R²=0.356
8. Quadratic: R²=-0.134 (fails)
9. Cubic: R²=-0.590 (fails)

Key insight: The analytic FORM is universal — 5th-degree
polynomial fits ALL coupling laws. But the COEFFICIENTS are
law-specific because each coupling function F(Δθ) produces
a different alignment-vs-R curve and a different F_max.

No simple 2-parameter model (power law, exponential, tanh)
captures the relationship adequately. The 5th-degree polynomial
is the simplest form that works across all laws. This suggests
the force-emergence process involves multiple nonlinear
interactions between phase alignment and coupling strength.

For cos specifically: Power law F = 0.98·R^5.4 gives R²=0.946 —
a remarkably simple approximate form.

### TQM-074

Alignment Order Parameter (408 profiles: 51 R-levels × 4 laws ×
2 seeds, 5 models compared).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION D: UNIVERSAL FORCE GENERATOR.

Best model: F = a · Alignment · ⟨f⟩ (Align × MeanForce)
R² = 0.9895 — near-perfect prediction.

Model comparison:
1. Align × MeanForce:  R² = 0.9895  (UNIVERSAL)
2. Coherence Poly5:   R² = 0.5986
3. Alignment + offset: R² = 0.5019
4. Alignment Direct:   R² = 0.4940
5. Align × PosArea:    R² = 0.3500

Per-law Align × MeanForce R²:
- cos:          0.9842
- cos²:         1.0000
- exp(-|x|):    1.0000
- 1/(1+|x|):    1.0000

Key insight: Alignment × MeanForce is the TRUE force equation.
It works across ALL coupling laws with near-perfect accuracy.
The formula is:
  F_net = a · (force alignment) · (mean local force magnitude)

Physically: net force = (fraction of forces aligned) × (typical
force per pair) × (number of pairs). Coherence R is merely a
precursor — it enables alignment, but alignment is the direct
force-generating variable.

For always-positive laws (cos², exp, 1/(1+|x|)), alignment is
always near 1.0 and force variation comes entirely from ⟨f⟩.
For cos, alignment varies with R and drives most of the force
variation, but ⟨f⟩ still contributes.

### TQM-075

Analytic Alignment Emergence (408 data points: 51 R-levels ×
4 laws × 2 seeds, 8 candidate models).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION A: NO ANALYTIC LAW (von Mises fails at finite N).

Alignment prediction — cos law:
1. Power Law A = 1.035·R^1.814:  R² = 0.9773  (best)
2. A = R²:                      R² = 0.9418  (best zero-param)
3. A = R:                       R² = 0.8413  (alignment ≠ coherence)
4. von Mises:                   R² = -1.0000 (fails: finite-N noise)

Key insight: A ≈ R² is the best ZERO-PARAMETER model (R²=0.942).
Alignment grows as the SQUARE of coherence, not linearly.
The von Mises analytic prediction overestimates alignment at
intermediate R because finite N=100 introduces phase noise
that reduces measured alignment below the infinite-N ideal.

Full analytic chain (using power law A(R)):
  P(θ|R) → A(R) → F_net(R)
  R² = 0.9773 × 0.9895 ≈ 0.967  — theory is CLOSED at >96%

Cancellation follows a similar pattern — derivable from phase
statistics but noisy at finite N.

The three-level force-emergence theory is now complete:
  Microscopic: P(θ|R) = vonMises(0, κ(R))
  Mesoscopic:  A(R) ≈ R²,  ⟨f⟩(R) from phase stats
  Macroscopic: F_net = a · A · ⟨f⟩

### TQM-076

Predictive Trajectory Theory (80 predictions: 10 R-levels ×
4 laws × 2 seeds, 50-step windows).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION B: MODERATE (always-positive laws).
CLASSIFICATION A: POOR (sign-changing laws).

Combined R²: -0.27 (poor — sign-changing laws dominate average).
Calibrated scale: a ≈ 133 (per-law scale factors vary widely).

Per-law trajectory prediction R² (A·⟨f⟩ → Δsep):
- cos:         -0.80  (POOR — phase evolution destroys prediction)
- cos²:         0.13  (weak)
- exp(-|x|):   0.51  (MODERATE)
- 1/(1+|x|):   0.69  (MODERATE — best)

Key insight: ALWAYS-POSITIVE coupling laws produce predictable
trajectories from initial conditions. Sign-changing laws (cos)
are unpredictable because alignment can change sign as phases
evolve during the trajectory.

This is a scientifically important finding: the three-level
theory (P(θ) → A → F → trajectory) works as a STATIC predictor
for always-positive coupling functions. For sign-changing
functions, the phase EVOLUTION during motion introduces
fundamental unpredictability — you need to know not just
initial phases but how they will evolve.

The theory is CLOSED for always-positive laws. For sign-changing
laws, it requires a DYNAMICAL extension (predicting phase
evolution, not just initial phases).

LEVEL 1 — Phase Distribution:
  P(θ|R) = vonMises(0, κ),  κ such that I₁(κ)/I₀(κ) = R

LEVEL 2 — Alignment & Mean Force:
  A(R) ≈ R²  (or A = a·Rⁿ with n≈1.8, R²=0.977)
  ⟨f⟩(R) = E[|F(θ)| | κ]  (analytic from coupling function)

LEVEL 3 — Net Force:
  F_net = a · A · ⟨f⟩  (R² = 0.9895)

Full chain: P(θ|R) → A(R) → F_net(R) with R² ≈ 0.967.

Key findings across TQM-062 to TQM-075:
- Attraction exists and is a vector summation phenomenon
- Coherence does not create force; it enables force alignment
- Alignment is the true macroscopic order parameter
- Alignment ≈ R² (not R) — a nonlinear emergence
- The complete theory can be closed from first principles

### TQM-080

Network Topology and Coherence Evolution (120 configs, 6 topology types).

Result:

Topology explains 63% of dR/dt variance beyond R alone.

- Best metric: MeanCoupling (ΔR² = +0.183)
- R²(R + MeanCoupling) = 0.739 vs R²(R only) = 0.555
- Topology is the dominant missing factor — larger than all hidden
  state variables from TQM-079 combined (ΔR² = +0.007)

### TQM-081

Effective Coupling Field (180 configs, 6 topology types, 7 topology metrics).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION D: EFFECTIVE COUPLING FIELD EXISTS.

- MeanCoupling alone retains 97.7% of full topology information
- R² jumps from 0.2645 (R only) → 0.7581 (R + MeanCoupling)
- Full topology (all 7 metrics): R² = 0.7696 — only +0.0115 more
- Adjusted R² favors the reduced model (0.7554 vs 0.7588 for full)
- MeanCoupling, MeanDegree, and CouplingEntropy are correlated >0.99
  — effectively the SAME variable measured differently

Key insight: THE DETAILED NETWORK IS NOT FUNDAMENTAL. A SINGLE
effective coupling-field variable (MeanCoupling) is sufficient to
capture all topology information relevant to dR/dt prediction.
The full coupling matrix can be compressed to its scalar mean with
negligible information loss.

This is a significant simplification for the TQM theory:
topology reduces to a scalar field — the mean coupling strength
experienced by each oscillator. The spatial arrangement
and network structure are epiphenomenal for coherence evolution.

### TQM-082

Mean Coupling Field Equation (72 profiles: 6 topologies × 3 K ×
2 λ × 2 seeds, 500-step simulations with phase+position dynamics).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION C: EFFECTIVE FIELD.

- M changes dramatically: mean |ΔM/M₀| = 413%, 100% of profiles
  show >1% change — M is a HIGHLY DYNAMICAL variable
- Best field equation: dM/dt = a₀ + a₁·M + a₂·R + a₃·M² + a₄·R² + a₅·M·R
  Adj R² = 0.299 (Model F, full quadratic)
- Model ranking: F (0.299) > B (0.262, M+M²) > D (0.225, M+R) >
  A (0.141, M only) > E (0.119, M·R) > C (0.000, R only)
- Pure R (Model C): R² ≈ 0 — M does NOT simply follow coherence
- M² terms add significant predictive power (B beats A by ΔR² = 0.12)

Key insight: M IS A TRUE DYNAMICAL FIELD VARIABLE — it does not
merely compress topology, it EVOLVES. The evolution is partially
captured by a quadratic field equation in (M, R). But the
predictability is modest (Adj R² = 0.30), suggesting higher-order
or stochastic effects dominate.

ASYMMETRIC COUPLING: R depends strongly on M (TQM-081, R² = 0.758)
but M depends only weakly on R (TQM-082, Adj R² = 0.299). The
effective theory has a ONE-WAY causal structure: M → R is strong,
R → M is weak. M is more fundamental than R for this system.

### TQM-083

Autonomous Theory Compression (9 candidate theories, 180 data points,
autonomous search with complexity-penalized scoring).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION C: UNIFIED REDUCED THEORY.

- **Minimal state variables: {R, M}** — only 2 variables capture
  all dynamical information from TQM-044 through TQM-082
- Theory ranking (after redundancy correction):
  {R, M}: Score 0.691, Mean Adj R² = 0.761
- **Discarded**: CouplingVariance, CouplingEntropy, SpectralGap,
  MeanDegree, SpatialClustering — all redundant
- **Alignment A**: Discarded as state variable — A ≈ R² is
  DERIVED, not independent
- **Memory β**: EXTERNAL PARAMETER, not state variable. Sets
  curvature (TQM-059) but does not emerge (TQM-061)
- **Force F_net**: DERIVED — F_net = A × ⟨f⟩ (TQM-074)
- Information loss from full model: -1.3% (minimal model is
  more efficient per degree of freedom)
- **Identity and Energy**: INDEPENDENT dimensions, not needed
  for coherence evolution

Governing equations:
  (1) dR/dt = α₀ + α₁·R + α₂·M          [Adj R² = 0.762]
  (2) dM/dt = β₀ + β₁·R + β₂·M          [Adj R² = 0.759]

Causal structure:
  β(fixed) → Curvature (static, does NOT drive motion)
  M ──strong──→ dR/dt  (R² = 0.758)
  R,M ──weak──→ dM/dt  (R² = 0.299)
  R → A≈R² → F_net = A·⟨f⟩  (derived, near-perfect)

Key insight: THE TQM SYSTEM IS A 2-VARIABLE EFFECTIVE FIELD THEORY.
M = ⟨K_ij⟩ is the effective coupling field — it is MORE FUNDAMENTAL
than coherence R. M determines R, but R only weakly feeds back to M.
This is analogous to gravitational potential (M) determining motion (R).

The network does not matter. Topology does not matter. Only one
scalar field matters: the mean coupling M.

### TQM-100

Physics Candidate Validation — hostile review of the {R, M} theory
(8 adversarial attack vectors, trained model vs extrapolation tests).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION A: THEORY REJECTED.

The {R, M} theory survived 3 of 8 attacks:

- **PASSED (3/8)**: Extreme M (R²=0.62), Mixed Topologies (R²=0.78),
  Different Coupling Laws (R²=0.78). M is genuinely universal across
  coupling functions and topologies — a MAJOR positive result.

- **FAILED (5/8)**: Extreme Coherence (R²=-1755), High Phase Noise
  (R²=-0.78), Large-N N=500 (R²=-3.72), Small-N N=10 (R²=-311),
  Out-of-Distribution (R²=-17.8).

Root causes:
1. **N-DEPENDENCE** (CRITICAL): dR/dt scales with N. Theory trained
   at N=100 breaks at N=10 and N=500. M alone does not capture N scaling.
2. **R SATURATION**: Linear model fails at R≈0 and R≈1. Need
   nonlinear R·(1-R) saturation term.
3. **NOISE SENSITIVITY**: Theory has no noise model.
4. **PARAMETER EXTRAPOLATION**: Theory fails when K,λ are far from
   training values.

Key insight: The theory is EXCELLENT within its training regime
(R²=0.73-0.78) but has ZERO extrapolation capability. The failure
is not in the {R, M} concept but in the specific functional form
(linear, no N-dependence).

The rejection is SCIENTIFICALLY PRODUCTIVE — it identifies exactly
what must be fixed: add N-dependence and R·(1-R) saturation.

### TQM-101

Theory Repair Program (7 repaired candidates... B: Partially Repaired, 4/8 attacks survived).

### TQM-102

Autonomous Equation Discovery (460-point dataset, 24 basis functions,
forward stepwise symbolic regression with AICc selection).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION B: IMPROVED EMPIRICAL MODEL.

- Discovered 9-term equation via AICc-optimal stepwise selection
- Training R² = 0.571, but only 3/8 attacks survived (38%)
- ACTUALLY WORSE than TQM-101 Model D (50% survival) — overfitting!

Selected terms: M, M², R·M, 1/N, N·M·R·(1-R), M³, M·R·(1-R), λ, N

What the regression correctly identified:
  • M, M², M³: coupling strength dominates (correct)
  • R·M: phase-coupling interaction matters (correct)
  • 1/N: finite-size correction (correct)
  • M·R·(1-R): logistic form near R≈0 and R≈1 (correct)
  • λ: spatial range matters (correct)

What went wrong: AICc with n=460 penalizes additional terms too
weakly (2 per term). The regression kept adding terms for marginal
AICc improvement (ΔAICc < 2 between S9 and S11), overfitting the
training data. BIC or cross-validation would produce a sparser model.

Key insight: THE CORRECT TERMS ARE IN THE LIBRARY but the selection
criterion needs to be stricter. A 3-term model (M, M², R·M) at
step 3 already has R²=0.476 — most of the predictive power comes
from just 3 coupling-related terms.

## Current Working Hypothesis

The data contains a simple underlying law (M-dominated with finite-size
and saturation corrections), but AICc-based symbolic regression
overfits. The true equation likely has 3-4 terms:

  dR/dt = a₁·M + a₂·M² + a₃·R·M + a₄·(1/N)

with possibly one logistic saturation term M·R·(1-R).

The state variables {R, M} remain correct. K and N appear only
through their effect on M (K determines M's magnitude; N determines
M's fluctuations via 1/N). The coupling law matters only through M.

## Next Open Question

Can we find a SPARSE equation (2-4 terms) that outperforms the
9-term overfit model? Specifically:

  (a) dR/dt = a₁·M + a₂·M²  (M-only, no R, no N)
  (b) dR/dt = a₁·M + a₂·M² + a₃·R·M  (M + interaction)
  (c) dR/dt = a₁·M + a₂·M² + a₃·(1/N)  (M + finite-size)
  (d) dR/dt = a₁·M·(1-R)  (simplest logistic)

Which sparse model best generalizes across the full parameter space?

### TQM-104

Mean-Field First-Principles Derivation (7 derived laws, 0–1 free
parameters, validated against all TQM-100 attacks).

Result:

CLASSIFICATION C: STRONG MEAN-FIELD THEORY.

- **MF-2 (M·R·(1-R²)/2): 4/8 survival, ZERO free parameters!**
  Pure derivation — no fitting. The functional form dR/dt ∝ M·R·(1-R²)
  is CORRECT and emerges naturally from the Kuramoto mean-field.
- **MF-7 (c₀·N·M·R·(1-R²)): 5/8 (62%) with one scale parameter**
  — BEST MODEL ACROSS ALL TQM-100..104 experiments!
- **Fitted c₀ = 0.0047**: The mean-field overestimates by ~100× because
  spatial heterogeneity reduces effective coupling. The FORM is right;
  the MAGNITUDE needs calibration.
- MF-1 (N·M form, 0/8): N·M overestimates — confirms TQM-103 that
  N-dependence is not simply Σ_j ≈ N·M.
- (1-R²) CONFIRMED over (1-R): MF-3 with (1-R) gets 0/8 vs MF-2's 4/8.

DERIVATION (not fitting):
  dR/dt = (1/N)Σᵢcos(θᵢ−ψ)·dθᵢ/dt
        = (1/N)ΣᵢΣⱼKᵢⱼ·cos(θᵢ−ψ)·sin(θⱼ−θᵢ)
  Mean-field → dR/dt = M·R·(1−R²)/2  [or more precisely c₀·M·R·(1−R²)]

Key insight: THE GOVERNING EQUATION IS DERIVABLE. The functional form
dR/dt ∝ M·R·(1−R²) is not an empirical fit — it follows from the
Kuramoto dynamics via mean-field theory. Only the overall coefficient
needs empirical calibration due to spatial heterogeneity.

The TQM system is described by a DERIVABLE effective theory:

where:

  c₀ ≈ 0.0047 (empirical, from spatial heterogeneity correction)

The (1−R²) factor is NOT assumed — it emerges from the mean-field
ensemble average. N appears through M's definition (M depends on
topology, K, λ, and weakly on N through finite-size effects).

This is the FIRST-PRINCIPLES effective theory for dR/dt.

Phase Portrait and Fixed Point Analysis of the closed theory.

Result:

- **Attractor**: (R,M)→(1,K) — 50/54 trajectories converge to R>0.999
- **No limit cycles**: both dR/dt≥0 and dM/dt≥0 — strictly monotonic
- **Exponential convergence**: ε(t)=1−R ∝ exp(−2c₀·M·t) near R=1
- **Self-reinforcing feedback**: R↑→M↑ (dM/dt∝R²), M↑→R↑ (dR/dt∝M)
- **Inevitable synchronization**: any R₀>0 → R→1, no exceptions

Physical interpretation: The closed theory predicts a SELF-REINFORCING
FIELD COLLAPSE to complete order. The only equilibrium (R=0) is
unstable — any perturbation triggers the cascade. This provides the
mathematical foundation for why synchronization is so robust
(TQM-052, TQM-053).

### TQM-107

Local Structure Survival (4 spatial scenarios, 5000 iterations,
position-dynamic Kuramoto with condensate detection).

Result: C: STRONG SPATIAL EFFECTS — mean-field breaks down for
multi-condensate systems.

- **Single condensate**: R→1.0, mean-field ADEQUATE ✓
- **Two condensates**: R stalls at 0.88, 2 condensates survive,
  Local R=1.0 within each, mean-field FAILS ✗
  The mean-field predicts dR/dt≈0.0007 but actual dR/dt≈0 —
  condensates are internally synchronized but phase-incoherent.
- **Multi (5 clusters)**: R→0.99, 4 condensates survive,
  mean-field holds (condensates close enough to merge) ✓
- **Random**: 6 condensates at R=0.81, mean-field FAILS ✗

Key insight: THE MEAN-FIELD THEORY IS CORRECT LOCALLY but fails
GLOBALLY for separated structures. Each condensate internally
synchronizes (R_local→1) exactly as the theory predicts. But
condensates separated beyond the coupling range (~3λ) cannot
phase-lock to each other. Global R depends on the relative phases
of condensates, which are random and frozen.

This resolves the apparent contradiction between TQM-106
(inevitable sync) and TQM-010-012 (persistent condensates):
the mean-field is a SINGLE-CONDENSATE theory. Multi-condensate
systems require spatial degrees of freedom beyond {R, M}.

### TQM-108

Spatial Field Theory Derivation — continuum limit of Kuramoto.

Result: D: TRUE FIELD THEORY WITH STABLE LOCALIZED STRUCTURES.

- Derived PDE from microscopic Kuramoto via continuum limit:
  ∂R/∂t = c₀·M·R·(1−R²) + D_R·∇²R
  ∂M/∂t = a·R²           + D_M·∇²M
  D_R ∝ λ²·M ≈ 2.5×10⁻⁵, D_M ∝ γ·λ² ≈ 2.5×10⁻⁶

- SOLITON SOLUTIONS: w ≈ √(2D_R/c₀·M) ≈ 0.10 (condensate width).
  Stable when separation > 3w ≈ 0.30. TQM-107 separation 0.6 > 0.30 ✓

- MEAN-FIELD RECOVERY: ODE is the ∇²→0 limit of PDE.
  Valid for single-condensate; fails for multi-condensate.

- UNIFICATION: The PDE explains ALL phenomena in one framework:
  TQM-104/105: ODE = homogeneous limit
  TQM-106:     Single-condensate attractor dynamics
  TQM-107:     Multi-condensate survival via diffusion prevention
  TQM-010-012: Proto-matter = field-theoretic solitons

- Proto-matter condensates are SOLITONS — localized stationary
  solutions of the spatial field equations. Each condensate is
  an INDEPENDENT LOCAL ATTRACTOR. The system has MULTIPLE
  attractors — one per spatially-separated condensate.

This is the definitive TQM theory: a REACTION-DIFFUSION PDE
derived from the microscopic Kuramoto dynamics, admitting
stable multi-soliton solutions.

## Current Working Hypothesis

The TQM system is a SPATIAL REACTION-DIFFUSION FIELD THEORY:

  ∂M/∂t = a·R² + D_M·∇²M               [clustering + coupling diffusion]

The ODE {dR/dt, dM/dt} is the spatially homogeneous (mean-field)
limit. Multi-condensate systems are MULTI-SOLITON solutions.
Proto-matter (TQM-010) = field-theoretic solitons.

  interference requires maintained Δφ
- Visibility data confirms cos(Δφ) modulation across phase sweep

linear wave superposition with interference fringes (wave aspect).

TQM is a theory with 2 irreducible primitives + 1 contingent number:

  Q (individuation) + Randomness (actualization) + M² (nonlinearity)
    → Time, Space, Spacetime, 3+1 dimensions
    → Hilbert space, Unitary QM, Schrödinger, Born, Measurement

    → G, ħ, c, Λ all derived from Q-event spacing ℓ

The only contingent continuous parameter is M² — the nonlinearity
regime of the effective PDE. All other apparent parameters (α, a₀,
γ, β_quark, β_lepton, ξ) are DERIVED from M² + the Q-event structure.

X060f proved: NO further reduction is possible. Q, Randomness, and M²
are all independently irreducible. The compression program is COMPLETE.
